I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about the memorial, even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It's... well, it's like my mind just won't shut off. My thoughts just keep jumping from one idea right to the next. \n\nOne minute I'm trying to figure out how to set up a display case, and then, before I know it, I'm planning out a road trip to track down a cousin, or I'm trying to remember a specific verse from an old ballad. It just goes and goes. I honestly can’t even slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like all these thoughts and activities for the memorial are a good thing, something you want to be doing?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix, I guess. I mean, one minute I feel completely lit up about it, you know? Just full of purpose, like I'm finally doing something important and the whole town is going to understand our family's history. But then... well, if someone gets in my way or questions what I'm doing, I just snap. Like my wife, or folks down at the senior center... if they slow me down or ask too many questions about the displays, I get real short with them. So I want to do it, yes, but it sure is making me irritable when people don't get with the program.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty about how you've been treating your wife and others who question your memorial plans?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean... I don't think I'm doing anything wrong. They're the ones getting in the way of something important. So, no, I don't feel guilty about it. I just want them to let me do my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a bad person for being so focused on the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm a bad person. Like I said, I'm just trying to get this done. It's important work, and I just need to focus on it. I don't see why that makes me bad.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial caused problems at home or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it has. I mean... I've stopped going to my walking group and the book club. I just haven't had the time, and I've let the garden go completely. And like I said about the senior center, I've definitely upset some friends down there because everything has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAt home... well, my wife says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she's always having to redirect me from making another phone call, or taking another trip, or getting into another argument with somebody about it. So, yes, it's definitely changed things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively guilty about anything unrelated to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt guilty about anything else. I mean, my mind is just so wrapped up in getting this project done, I don't really have the time or energy to worry about much else. So, no, no guilty feelings like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I just want them to let me do my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a bad person for being so focused on the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm a bad person. Like I said, I'm just trying to get this done. It's important work, and I just need to focus on it. I don't see why that makes me bad.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial caused problems at home or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it has. I mean... I've stopped going to my walking group and the book club. I just haven't had the time, and I've let the garden go completely. And like I said about the senior center, I've definitely upset some friends down there because everything has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAt home... well, my wife says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she's always having to redirect me from making another phone call, or taking another trip, or getting into another argument with somebody about it. So, yes, it's definitely changed things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively guilty about anything unrelated to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt guilty about anything else. I mean, my mind is just so wrapped up in getting this project done, I don't really have the time or energy to worry about much else. So, no, no guilty feelings like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling unhappy or distressed about the way you've been acting and the impact it's having on your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I know my wife is upset by it all, but I don't feel distressed myself. I'm just doing what needs to be done. It's a very important project, so... no, I don't feel unhappy about the way I'm acting. I'm just focused.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping because of all the thoughts and activities related to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble sleeping. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I might be up a bit later working on things, or wake up thinking about the next step for the display, but it's not like I'm tossing and turning or anything. When I lay down, I sleep. I just... I don't need as much sleep right now because there's so much to do. But it's not a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a very specific way for the memorial, or that something bad will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I want it to be done right, of course, because it's an important project. But... I don't have any strict rules I have to follow, and I don't think anything bad is going to happen if I do something out of order. I just want to get it finished. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfectly symmetrical or arranged in a specific way for it to be right?\n\nPatient: No, not perfectly symmetrical or anything like that. I mean, it's a memorial, so it needs to look good and be respectful, but... no, there's no specific pattern it has to follow. I'm just putting the pieces together as they make sense to me. It's more about getting the message across than making sure every little thing lines up perfectly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing the memorial the right way, or is there a fear that if it's not done a certain way, it won't be good enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about that. I know I'm doing it right. I mean, it's my project, so I know how it's supposed to look. I don't have any fear about it not being good enough. It's going to be great when it's done, I'm sure of it. I just need to keep working on it and get it finished, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to control every detail of the memorial yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I'm the one making it, so naturally I'm the one handling the pieces. But I don't feel like I have to control every single little thing about it. If somebody wanted to help out or hand me materials, that'd be fine by me. I just know what the message is supposed to be, so I'm doing the work to get it built. That's pretty much it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my wife is the main reason I'm here. See, I've been putting together a memorial for my brother. It's just... it's gotten a lot bigger than I originally meant it to. \n\nI've been spending most of my days chasing down the right songs, calling folks up, planning out all these displays and even looking into some trips for it. My wife says I’ve completely pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and just about everything else I usually do. So, she wanted me to come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do all these things for the memorial, like you can't stop even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I suppose so. It's just... I can't seem to sit in my chair like I used to. I just can't stay put. I'm always up hauling boxes around, making phone calls, rearranging all the papers for the displays... just walking from room to room. Honestly, I feel like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about the memorial, even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It's... well, it's like my mind just won't shut off. My thoughts just keep jumping from one idea right to the next. \n\nOne minute I'm trying to figure out how to set up a display case, and then, before I know it, I'm planning out a road trip to track down a cousin, or I'm trying to remember a specific verse from an old ballad. It just goes and goes. I honestly can’t even slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like all these thoughts and activities for the memorial are a good thing, something you want to be doing?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix, I guess. I mean, one minute I feel completely lit up about it, you know? Just full of purpose, like I'm finally doing something important and the whole town is going to understand our family's history. But then... well, if someone gets in my way or questions what I'm doing, I just snap. Like my wife, or folks down at the senior center... if they slow me down or ask too many questions about the displays, I get real short with them. So I want to do it, yes, but it sure is making me irritable when people don't get with the program.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty about how you've been treating your wife and others who question your memorial plans?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean... I don't think I'm doing anything wrong. They're the ones getting in the way of something important. So, no, I don't feel guilty about it. I just want them to let me do my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a bad person for being so focused on the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm a bad person. Like I said, I'm just trying to get this done. It's important work, and I just need to focus on it. I don't see why that makes me bad.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial caused problems at home or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it has. I mean... I've stopped going to my walking group and the book club. I just haven't had the time, and I've let the garden go completely. And like I said about the senior center, I've definitely upset some friends down there because everything has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAt home... well, my wife says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she's always having to redirect me from making another phone call, or taking another trip, or getting into another argument with somebody about it. So, yes, it's definitely changed things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively guilty about anything unrelated to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt guilty about anything else. I mean, my mind is just so wrapped up in getting this project done, I don't really have the time or energy to worry about much else. So, no, no guilty feelings like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling unhappy or distressed about the way you've been acting and the impact it's having on your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I know my wife is upset by it all, but I don't feel distressed myself. I'm just doing what needs to be done. It's a very important project, so... no, I don't feel unhappy about the way I'm acting. I'm just focused.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping because of all the thoughts and activities related to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble sleeping. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I might be up a bit later working on things, or wake up thinking about the next step for the display, but it's not like I'm tossing and turning or anything. When I lay down, I sleep. I just... I don't need as much sleep right now because there's so much to do. But it's not a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a very specific way for the memorial, or that something bad will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I want it to be done right, of course, because it's an important project. But... I don't have any strict rules I have to follow, and I don't think anything bad is going to happen if I do something out of order. I just want to get it finished. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfectly symmetrical or arranged in a specific way for it to be right?\n\nPatient: No, not perfectly symmetrical or anything like that. I mean, it's a memorial, so it needs to look good and be respectful, but... no, there's no specific pattern it has to follow. I'm just putting the pieces together as they make sense to me. It's more about getting the message across than making sure every little thing lines up perfectly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing the memorial the right way, or is there a fear that if it's not done a certain way, it won't be good enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about that. I know I'm doing it right. I mean, it's my project, so I know how it's supposed to look. I don't have any fear about it not being good enough. It's going to be great when it's done, I'm sure of it. I just need to keep working on it and get it finished, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to control every detail of the memorial yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I'm the one making it, so naturally I'm the one handling the pieces. But I don't feel like I have to control every single little thing about it. If somebody wanted to help out or hand me materials, that'd be fine by me. I just know what the message is supposed to be, so I'm doing the work to get it built. That's pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfect in any way?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't have to be perfect. I put things where they make sense to me. There's no strict rule about it or anything. If a piece looks good in one spot, I leave it there. It's just about getting the message out, not following some kind of exact blueprint. So, no, I wouldn't say there's a right or wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be extremely upset if someone else were to make changes to your memorial without your input?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't say extremely upset. I mean, I guess I'd want to know why they were messing with it without asking me first, since I'm the one building it. But... as long as they didn't ruin the message or disrespect the people it's for, I wouldn't throw a fit or anything. Like I said, it's just about getting the point across, so if what they added fits, it wouldn't really bother me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just... it's gotten a lot bigger than I originally meant it to. \n\nI've been spending most of my days chasing down the right songs, calling folks up, planning out all these displays and even looking into some trips for it. My wife says I’ve completely pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and just about everything else I usually do. So, she wanted me to come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do all these things for the memorial, like you can't stop even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I suppose so. It's just... I can't seem to sit in my chair like I used to. I just can't stay put. I'm always up hauling boxes around, making phone calls, rearranging all the papers for the displays... just walking from room to room. Honestly, I feel like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about the memorial, even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It's... well, it's like my mind just won't shut off. My thoughts just keep jumping from one idea right to the next. \n\nOne minute I'm trying to figure out how to set up a display case, and then, before I know it, I'm planning out a road trip to track down a cousin, or I'm trying to remember a specific verse from an old ballad. It just goes and goes. I honestly can’t even slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like all these thoughts and activities for the memorial are a good thing, something you want to be doing?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix, I guess. I mean, one minute I feel completely lit up about it, you know? Just full of purpose, like I'm finally doing something important and the whole town is going to understand our family's history. But then... well, if someone gets in my way or questions what I'm doing, I just snap. Like my wife, or folks down at the senior center... if they slow me down or ask too many questions about the displays, I get real short with them. So I want to do it, yes, but it sure is making me irritable when people don't get with the program.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty about how you've been treating your wife and others who question your memorial plans?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean... I don't think I'm doing anything wrong. They're the ones getting in the way of something important. So, no, I don't feel guilty about it. I just want them to let me do my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a bad person for being so focused on the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm a bad person. Like I said, I'm just trying to get this done. It's important work, and I just need to focus on it. I don't see why that makes me bad.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial caused problems at home or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it has. I mean... I've stopped going to my walking group and the book club. I just haven't had the time, and I've let the garden go completely. And like I said about the senior center, I've definitely upset some friends down there because everything has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAt home... well, my wife says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she's always having to redirect me from making another phone call, or taking another trip, or getting into another argument with somebody about it. So, yes, it's definitely changed things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively guilty about anything unrelated to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt guilty about anything else. I mean, my mind is just so wrapped up in getting this project done, I don't really have the time or energy to worry about much else. So, no, no guilty feelings like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling unhappy or distressed about the way you've been acting and the impact it's having on your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I know my wife is upset by it all, but I don't feel distressed myself. I'm just doing what needs to be done. It's a very important project, so... no, I don't feel unhappy about the way I'm acting. I'm just focused.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping because of all the thoughts and activities related to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble sleeping. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I might be up a bit later working on things, or wake up thinking about the next step for the display, but it's not like I'm tossing and turning or anything. When I lay down, I sleep. I just... I don't need as much sleep right now because there's so much to do. But it's not a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a very specific way for the memorial, or that something bad will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I want it to be done right, of course, because it's an important project. But... I don't have any strict rules I have to follow, and I don't think anything bad is going to happen if I do something out of order. I just want to get it finished. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfectly symmetrical or arranged in a specific way for it to be right?\n\nPatient: No, not perfectly symmetrical or anything like that. I mean, it's a memorial, so it needs to look good and be respectful, but... no, there's no specific pattern it has to follow. I'm just putting the pieces together as they make sense to me. It's more about getting the message across than making sure every little thing lines up perfectly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing the memorial the right way, or is there a fear that if it's not done a certain way, it won't be good enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about that. I know I'm doing it right. I mean, it's my project, so I know how it's supposed to look. I don't have any fear about it not being good enough. It's going to be great when it's done, I'm sure of it. Like I said, it's just about getting the point across, so if what they added fits, it wouldn't really bother me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial needs to be arranged in a specific order to feel right to you?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't need to be in any specific order. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my wife is the main reason I'm here. See, I've been putting together a memorial for my brother. It's just... it's gotten a lot bigger than I originally meant it to. \n\nI've been spending most of my days chasing down the right songs, calling folks up, planning out all these displays and even looking into some trips for it. My wife says I’ve completely pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and just about everything else I usually do. So, she wanted me to come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do all these things for the memorial, like you can't stop even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I suppose so. It's just... I can't seem to sit in my chair like I used to. I just can't stay put. I'm always up hauling boxes around, making phone calls, rearranging all the papers for the displays... just walking from room to room. Honestly, I feel like my body has more steam in it right now than a man my age ought to have. I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about the memorial, even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It's... well, it's like my mind just won't shut off. My thoughts just keep jumping from one idea right to the next. \n\nOne minute I'm trying to figure out how to set up a display case, and then, before I know it, I'm planning out a road trip to track down a cousin, or I'm trying to remember a specific verse from an old ballad. It just goes and goes. I honestly can’t even slow my head down enough to write any of it out in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like all these thoughts and activities for the memorial are a good thing, something you want to be doing?\n\nPatient: Well, it's a mix, I guess. I mean, one minute I feel completely lit up about it, you know? Just full of purpose, like I'm finally doing something important and the whole town is going to understand our family's history. But then... well, if someone gets in my way or questions what I'm doing, I just snap. Like my wife, or folks down at the senior center... if they slow me down or ask too many questions about the displays, I get real short with them. So I want to do it, yes, but it sure is making me irritable when people don't get with the program.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty about how you've been treating your wife and others who question your memorial plans?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean... I don't think I'm doing anything wrong. They're the ones getting in the way of something important. So, no, I don't feel guilty about it. I just want them to let me do my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a bad person for being so focused on the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm a bad person. Like I said, I'm just trying to get this done. It's important work, and I just need to focus on it. I don't see why that makes me bad.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial caused problems at home or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it has. I mean... I've stopped going to my walking group and the book club. I just haven't had the time, and I've let the garden go completely. And like I said about the senior center, I've definitely upset some friends down there because everything has just become about this memorial display. \n\nAt home... well, my wife says our whole day just revolves around her trying to calm me down. She tells me she's always having to redirect me from making another phone call, or taking another trip, or getting into another argument with somebody about it. So, yes, it's definitely changed things around the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively guilty about anything unrelated to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt guilty about anything else. I mean, my mind is just so wrapped up in getting this project done, I don't really have the time or energy to worry about much else. So, no, no guilty feelings like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling unhappy or distressed about the way you've been acting and the impact it's having on your life?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I know my wife is upset by it all, but I don't feel distressed myself. I'm just doing what needs to be done. It's a very important project, so... no, I don't feel unhappy about the way I'm acting. I'm just focused.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping because of all the thoughts and activities related to the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble sleeping. I mean, I'm sleeping just fine, really. I might be up a bit later working on things, or wake up thinking about the next step for the display, but it's not like I'm tossing and turning or anything. When I lay down, I sleep. I just... I don't need as much sleep right now because there's so much to do. But it's not a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a very specific way for the memorial, or that something bad will happen if you don't?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I want it to be done right, of course, because it's an important project. But... I don't have any strict rules I have to follow, and I don't think anything bad is going to happen if I do something out of order. I just want to get it finished. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfectly symmetrical or arranged in a specific way for it to be right?\n\nPatient: No, not perfectly symmetrical or anything like that. I mean, it's a memorial, so it needs to look good and be respectful, but... no, there's no specific pattern it has to follow. I'm just putting the pieces together as they make sense to me. It's more about getting the message across than making sure every little thing lines up perfectly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing the memorial the right way, or is there a fear that if it's not done a certain way, it won't be good enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about that. I know I'm doing it right. I mean, it's my project, so I know how it's supposed to look. I don't have any fear about it not being good enough. It's going to be great when it's done, I'm sure of it. I just need to keep working on it and get it finished, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to control every detail of the memorial yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I'm the one making it, so naturally I'm the one handling the pieces. But I don't feel like I have to control every single little thing about it. If somebody wanted to help out or hand me materials, that'd be fine by me. I just know what the message is supposed to be, so I'm doing the work to get it built. That's pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial has to be perfect in any way?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't have to be perfect. I mean, I want it to look nice, but it's not like I'm obsessing over every little detail being flawless. It just needs to get the point across and honor the people it's meant for. As long as it does that, it's fine by me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's a right or wrong way to organize the items in the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I put things where they make sense to me. There's no strict rule about it or anything. If a piece looks good in one spot, I leave it there. It's just about getting the message out, not following some kind of exact blueprint. So, no, I wouldn't say there's a right or wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be extremely upset if someone else were to make changes to your memorial without your input?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't say extremely upset. I mean, I guess I'd want to know why they were messing with it without asking me first, since I'm the one building it. But... as long as they didn't ruin the message or disrespect the people it's for, I wouldn't throw a fit or anything. Like I said, it's just about getting the point across, so if what they added fits, it wouldn't really bother me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial needs to be arranged in a specific order to feel right to you?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't need to be in any specific order. Like I was saying, I just put things where they seem to fit at the time. I'm not following a list or trying to make it look a certain way from left to right or anything like that. As long as it all comes together to share the message, the exact order doesn't matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something bad would happen if you couldn't work on the memorial for a day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think anything bad would happen. I just need to keep working on it and get it finished, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to control every detail of the memorial yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. As long as it does that, it's fine by me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's a right or wrong way to organize the items in the memorial?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I put things where they make sense to me. There's no strict rule about it or anything. If a piece looks good in one spot, I leave it there. It's just about getting the message out, not following some kind of exact blueprint. So, no, I wouldn't say there's a right or wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be extremely upset if someone else were to make changes to your memorial without your input?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't say extremely upset. I mean, I guess I'd want to know why they were messing with it without asking me first, since I'm the one building it. But... as long as they didn't ruin the message or disrespect the people it's for, I wouldn't throw a fit or anything. Like I said, it's just about getting the point across, so if what they added fits, it wouldn't really bother me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial needs to be arranged in a specific order to feel right to you?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't need to be in any specific order. Like I was saying, I just put things where they seem to fit at the time. I'm not following a list or trying to make it look a certain way from left to right or anything like that. As long as it all comes together to share the message, the exact order doesn't matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something bad would happen if you couldn't work on the memorial for a day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think anything bad would happen. I mean, I wouldn't want to stop working on it, because I want to get it finished, but... no, there's no bad luck or anything tied to it. If I had to take a day off, I'd just pick it back up the next day. It's important to me, but it's not like the world's going to end if I don't touch it for 24 hours.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, one minute I feel completely lit up about it, you know? Just full of purpose, like I'm finally doing something important and the whole town is going to understand our family's history. But then... well, if someone gets in my way or questions what I'm doing, I just snap. I mean... I don't think I'm doing anything wrong. They're the ones getting in the way of something important. So, no, I don't feel guilty about it.
